To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes, we need to identify all paths between these nodes and remove the minimum number of edges that eliminate all such paths.

The two black nodes are labeled **G** and **I**.

### Identifying Paths Between G and I:

1. **Direct Paths:**
   - (G, F), (F, X), (X, J), (J, I)
   - (G, F), (F, K), (K, J), (J, I)

2. **Indirect Paths:**
   - (G, F), (F, X), (X, K), (K, J), (J, I)
   - (G, F), (F, M), (M, L), (L, E), (E, B), (B, N), (N, Y), (Y, O), (O, C), (C, I)
   - (G, H), (H, B), (B, N), (N, Y), (Y, O), (O, C), (C, I)
   - (G, H), (H, B), (B, N), (N, C), (C, I)
   - (G, H), (H, B), (B, N), (N, Y), (Y, O), (O, P), (P, J), (J, I)
   - (G, H), (H, B), (B, N), (N, C), (C, P), (P, J), (J, I)
   - (G, H), (H, B), (B, N), (N, C), (C, P), (P, D), (D, J), (J, I)

### Finding the Cut Set:

To disconnect G and I, we need to remove edges such that all paths between them are eliminated. The key paths to consider are those that must be cut to ensure no remaining connection:

1. Remove (G, H) to break the path through H.
2. Remove (B, N) to break multiple paths through B and N.
3. Remove (J, I) to directly break the connection between J and I.

By removing these three edges, all paths between G and I are eliminated.

### Conclusion:

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(G, H), (B, N), (J, I)].
